#engine/core/input.py
# engine/core/input.py
from __future__ import annotations

import pygame

from engine.utils.logger import Logger


class Input:
    _pressed_keys = set()
    _keys_down = set()
    _keys_up = set()
    _mouse_x = 0
    _mouse_y = 0
    _left_pressed = False
    _left_clicked = False

    @classmethod
    def initialize(cls):
        cls.reset()
        Logger.info("[Input] Initialized")

    @classmethod
    def reset(cls):
        cls._pressed_keys.clear()
        cls._keys_down.clear()
        cls._keys_up.clear()
        cls._mouse_x = 0
        cls._mouse_y = 0
        cls._left_pressed = False
        cls._left_clicked = False

    @classmethod
    def update(cls, events):
        cls._keys_down.clear()
        cls._keys_up.clear()
        cls._left_clicked = False
        cls._mouse_x, cls._mouse_y = pygame.mouse.get_pos()

        for event in events:
            if event.type == pygame.KEYDOWN:
                cls._pressed_keys.add(event.key)
                cls._keys_down.add(event.key)
            elif event.type == pygame.KEYUP:
                cls._pressed_keys.discard(event.key)
                cls._keys_up.add(event.key)
            elif event.type == pygame.MOUSEBUTTONDOWN:
                if event.button == 1:
                    cls._left_pressed = True
                    cls._left_clicked = True
            elif event.type == pygame.MOUSEBUTTONUP:
                if event.button == 1:
                    cls._left_pressed = False
            elif event.type == getattr(pygame, "WINDOWFOCUSLOST", -1):
                cls._pressed_keys.clear()
                cls._left_pressed = False
            elif event.type == pygame.ACTIVEEVENT:
                if getattr(event, "gain", 1) == 0:
                    cls._pressed_keys.clear()
                    cls._left_pressed = False

    @classmethod
    def is_key_pressed(cls, key):
        return key in cls._pressed_keys

    @classmethod
    def is_key_clicked(cls, key):
        return key in cls._keys_down

    @classmethod
    def get_mouse_position(cls):
        return (cls._mouse_x, cls._mouse_y)

    @classmethod
    def get_mouse_x(cls):
        return cls._mouse_x

    @classmethod
    def get_mouse_y(cls):
        return cls._mouse_y

    @classmethod
    def is_left_mouse_pressed(cls):
        return cls._left_pressed

    @classmethod
    def is_left_mouse_clicked(cls):
        return cls._left_clicked

    @classmethod
    def is_key_released(cls, key):
        return key in cls._keys_up

#engine/core/renderer.py
# engine/core/renderer.py
import moderngl

from engine.rendering.camera2d import Camera2D
from engine.rendering.overlay_renderer import OverlayRenderer
from engine.rendering.primitive_renderer import PrimitiveRenderer
from engine.rendering.text_renderer import TextRenderer
from engine.utils.logger import Logger


class Renderer:
    def __init__(self):
        self.ctx = None
        self.camera = None
        self.primitives = None
        self.text = None
        self.overlay = None

    def initialize(
        self,
        width: int,
        height: int,
    ):
        self.ctx = moderngl.create_context()
        self.ctx.enable(moderngl.BLEND)
        self.ctx.blend_func = (
            moderngl.SRC_ALPHA,
            moderngl.ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA,
        )
        self.overlay = OverlayRenderer(self)
        Logger.info(f"[Renderer] OpenGL Version: {self.ctx.info['GL_VERSION']}")
        self.camera = Camera2D(width, height)
        self.primitives = PrimitiveRenderer(
            self.ctx,
            self.camera,
        )
        self.text = TextRenderer(
            self.ctx,
            self.camera,
        )
        Logger.info("[Renderer] Rendering system initialized.")

    def begin_frame(self):
        self.ctx.clear(
            red=0.08,
            green=0.08,
            blue=0.10,
            alpha=1.0,
        )
        self.primitives.begin_frame()

    def end_frame(self):
        self.primitives.end_frame()

    def draw_line(
        self,
        start,
        end,
        color,
    ):
        self.primitives.draw_line(
            start,
            end,
            color,
        )

    def draw_rectangle(
        self,
        position,
        size,
        color,
    ):
        self.primitives.draw_rectangle(
            position,
            size,
            color,
        )

    def draw_filled_rectangle(
        self,
        position,
        size,
        color,
    ):
        self.primitives.draw_filled_rectangle(
            position,
            size,
            color,
        )

    def draw_circle(
        self,
        center,
        radius,
        color,
        segments=64,
    ):
        self.primitives.draw_circle(
            center,
            radius,
            color,
            segments,
        )

    def draw_filled_circle(
        self,
        center,
        radius,
        color,
        segments=64,
    ):
        self.primitives.draw_filled_circle(
            center,
            radius,
            color,
            segments,
        )

    def draw_cross(
        self,
        center,
        size,
        color,
    ):
        self.primitives.draw_cross(
            center,
            size,
            color,
        )

    def draw_grid(
        self,
        origin,
        rows,
        columns,
        cell_size,
        color,
    ):
        self.primitives.draw_grid(
            origin,
            rows,
            columns,
            cell_size,
            color,
        )

    def draw_text(
        self,
        text,
        position,
        size=32,
        color=(255, 255, 255),
        font_name=None,
    ):
        self.text.draw_text(
            text=text,
            position=position,
            size=size,
            color=color,
            font_name=font_name,
        )

    def draw_overlay_message(
        self,
        message,
        color=(255, 255, 255),
        size=56,
    ):
        self.overlay.draw_message(
            message=message,
            color=color,
            size=size,
        )

    def resize(
        self,
        width,
        height,
    ):
        self.camera.resize(
            width,
            height,
        )

    def shutdown(self):
        if self.primitives is not None:
            self.primitives.release()
        if self.text is not None:
            self.text.release()
        if self.overlay is not None:
            self.overlay.release()
        if self.ctx is not None:
            self.ctx.release()
        Logger.info("[Renderer] Shutdown")

#engine/core/scene_manager.py
# engine/core/scene_manager.py
class SceneManager:
    def __init__(self, game_manager):
        self.game_manager = game_manager
        self.current_scene = None

    def change_scene(self, scene):
        if self.current_scene is not None:
            self.current_scene.exit()
        self.current_scene = scene
        self.current_scene.set_game_manager(self.game_manager)
        self.current_scene.enter()

    def update(self):
        if self.current_scene is not None:
            self.current_scene.update()

    def render(self):
        if self.current_scene is not None:
            self.current_scene.render()

    def get_current_scene(self):
        return self.current_scene

# engine/core/scene.py
from engine.utils.logger import Logger


class Scene:
    def __init__(self, name):
        self.name = name
        self.game_manager = None
        self.scene_manager = None
        self.window = None
        self.renderer = None
        self.time_manager = None

    def set_game_manager(self, game_manager):
        self.game_manager = game_manager
        self.scene_manager = game_manager.scene_manager
        self.window = game_manager.window
        self.renderer = game_manager.renderer
        self.time_manager = game_manager.time_manager

    def enter(self):
        Logger.info(f"[Scene] Enter: {self.name}")

    def exit(self):
        Logger.info(f"[Scene] Exit: {self.name}")

    def update(self):
        pass

    def render(self):
        pass

#engine/core/time.py
# engine/core/time.py
import time


class TimeManager:
    def __init__(self):
        self.delta_time = 0.0
        self.current_time = time.perf_counter()
        self.previous_time = self.current_time
        self.fps = 0
        self.frame_count = 0
        self.fps_timer = 0.0
        self.total_runtime = 0.0

    def update(self):
        self.current_time = time.perf_counter()
        self.delta_time = self.current_time - self.previous_time
        self.previous_time = self.current_time
        self.total_runtime += self.delta_time
        self.frame_count += 1
        self.fps_timer += self.delta_time
        if self.fps_timer >= 1.0:
            self.fps = self.frame_count
            self.frame_count = 0
            self.fps_timer = 0.0

    def get_delta_time(self):
        return self.delta_time

    def get_fps(self):
        return self.fps

    def get_runtime(self):
        return self.total_runtime

#engine/core/window.py
# engine/core/window.py
import pygame

from engine.utils.logger import Logger


class Window:
    def __init__(self, width, height, title):
        self.width = width
        self.height = height
        self.title = title
        self.screen = None
        self.clock = None

    def create(self):
        pygame.init()
        pygame.display.gl_set_attribute(pygame.GL_CONTEXT_MAJOR_VERSION, 3)
        pygame.display.gl_set_attribute(pygame.GL_CONTEXT_MINOR_VERSION, 3)
        pygame.display.gl_set_attribute(
            pygame.GL_CONTEXT_PROFILE_MASK, pygame.GL_CONTEXT_PROFILE_CORE
        )
        self.screen = pygame.display.set_mode(
            (self.width, self.height), pygame.OPENGL | pygame.DOUBLEBUF
        )
        pygame.display.set_caption(self.title)
        self.clock = pygame.time.Clock()
        Logger.info(f"[Window] Created {self.width}x{self.height}")

    def update(self):
        pygame.display.flip()

    def tick(self, fps=60):
        self.clock.tick(fps)

    def destroy(self):
        pygame.quit()
        Logger.info("[Window] Destroyed")

#engine/core/animation.py
# engine/core/animation.py
"""Animation trajectory system for piece movements.

Exposes trajectory generators for common board-game animations:
slides, arcs (knight jumps), drops (Connect Four), and flips (Othello).
Each function yields ``(x, y, progress)`` tuples that the renderer
can sample at 60 FPS.

Usage::

    traj = ArcTrajectory(
        start=(100, 400), end=(300, 200),
        arc_height=80, duration=0.4,
    )
    traj.start()
    while not traj.is_done():
        x, y = traj.get_position(delta_time)
        draw_piece(x, y)
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

from engine.utils.helpers import clamp, ease_in_out_cubic, ease_out_bounce, lerp

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Base Trajectory
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


@dataclass
class Trajectory:
    """Base class for all animation trajectories.

    Attributes:
        start: Starting screen position ``(x, y)``.
        end: Ending screen position ``(x, y)``.
        duration: Total animation time in seconds.
    """

    start: tuple[float, float] = (0.0, 0.0)
    end: tuple[float, float] = (0.0, 0.0)
    duration: float = 0.3

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        self._elapsed: float = 0.0
        self._active: bool = False

    def start_animation(self) -> None:
        """Begin the animation from time zero."""
        self._elapsed = 0.0
        self._active = True

    def reset(self) -> None:
        """Reset the animation to its initial state."""
        self._elapsed = 0.0
        self._active = False

    def is_active(self) -> bool:
        """Return ``True`` if the animation is currently playing."""
        return self._active

    def is_done(self) -> bool:
        """Return ``True`` if the animation has finished."""
        return self._elapsed >= self.duration

    def get_progress(self) -> float:
        """Return linear progress in ``[0, 1]``."""
        if self.duration <= 0:
            return 1.0
        return clamp(self._elapsed / self.duration, 0.0, 1.0)

    def advance(self, delta_time: float) -> None:
        """Advance the animation clock.

        Args:
            delta_time: Seconds since last frame.
        """
        if not self._active:
            return
        self._elapsed += delta_time
        if self._elapsed >= self.duration:
            self._elapsed = self.duration
            self._active = False

    def get_position(self, delta_time: float = 0.0) -> tuple[float, float]:
        """Advance by *delta_time* and return the current position.

        Args:
            delta_time: Seconds since last call (0 to just query).

        Returns:
            ``(x, y)`` screen position.
        """
        self.advance(delta_time)
        t = ease_in_out_cubic(self.get_progress())
        x = lerp(self.start[0], self.end[0], t)
        y = lerp(self.start[1], self.end[1], t)
        return (x, y)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Slide (linear movement)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


@dataclass
class SlideTrajectory(Trajectory):
    """Straight-line slide from *start* to *end*.

    Used for rook moves, pawn pushes, checkers slides.
    """

    def get_position(self, delta_time: float = 0.0) -> tuple[float, float]:
        self.advance(delta_time)
        t = ease_in_out_cubic(self.get_progress())
        x = lerp(self.start[0], self.end[0], t)
        y = lerp(self.start[1], self.end[1], t)
        return (x, y)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Arc (parabolic jump)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


@dataclass
class ArcTrajectory(Trajectory):
    """Parabolic arc from *start* to *end* with a peak height.

    Used for knight jumps, checker captures.

    Attributes:
        arc_height: Maximum height of the arc above the straight line (px).
    """

    arc_height: float = 50.0

    def get_position(self, delta_time: float = 0.0) -> tuple[float, float]:
        self.advance(delta_time)
        t = ease_in_out_cubic(self.get_progress())
        x = lerp(self.start[0], self.end[0], t)
        base_y = lerp(self.start[1], self.end[1], t)
        # Parabolic offset: peaks at t=0.5
        arc_offset = -4.0 * self.arc_height * t * (1.0 - t)
        y = base_y + arc_offset
        return (x, y)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Drop (gravity / bounce)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


@dataclass
class DropTrajectory(Trajectory):
    """Vertical drop with bounce easing — for Connect Four disc drops.

    The piece starts at ``start`` (top of the column) and drops to
    ``end`` (the landing row) with a bouncing finish.
    """

    def get_position(self, delta_time: float = 0.0) -> tuple[float, float]:
        self.advance(delta_time)
        t = ease_out_bounce(self.get_progress())
        x = lerp(self.start[0], self.end[0], t)
        y = lerp(self.start[1], self.end[1], t)
        return (x, y)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Flip (Othello disc flip)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


@dataclass
class FlipTrajectory(Trajectory):
    """Scale-based flip animation for Othello disc reversals.

    The disc scales down to zero width at the midpoint (simulating a
    3D flip), then scales back up with the new colour.

    Attributes:
        flip_axis: ``'x'`` for horizontal flip, ``'y'`` for vertical.
    """

    flip_axis: str = "x"

    def get_scale(self, delta_time: float = 0.0) -> tuple[float, float]:
        """Return ``(scale_x, scale_y)`` representing the disc's visible size.

        At the midpoint one axis collapses to 0 (edge-on view).

        Args:
            delta_time: Seconds since last call.

        Returns:
            Scale factors for x and y.
        """
        self.advance(delta_time)
        t = self.get_progress()
        # First half: shrink to edge; second half: expand back
        if t < 0.5:
            factor = 1.0 - 2.0 * t
        else:
            factor = 2.0 * t - 1.0

        if self.flip_axis == "x":
            return (factor, 1.0)
        return (1.0, factor)

    def get_position(self, delta_time: float = 0.0) -> tuple[float, float]:
        """Flip stays in place — returns the midpoint of start/end."""
        self.advance(delta_time)
        x = (self.start[0] + self.end[0]) * 0.5
        y = (self.start[1] + self.end[1]) * 0.5
        return (x, y)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Fade (appear / disappear)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


@dataclass
class FadeTrajectory(Trajectory):
    """Opacity fade for piece captures (fade out) or placements (fade in).

    Attributes:
        fade_in: ``True`` for 0→1 opacity; ``False`` for 1→0.
    """

    fade_in: bool = True

    def get_opacity(self, delta_time: float = 0.0) -> float:
        """Return current opacity ``[0.0 … 1.0]``.

        Args:
            delta_time: Seconds since last call.

        Returns:
            Opacity float.
        """
        self.advance(delta_time)
        t = ease_in_out_cubic(self.get_progress())
        if self.fade_in:
            return t
        return 1.0 - t

    def get_position(self, delta_time: float = 0.0) -> tuple[float, float]:
        self.advance(delta_time)
        return self.start


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Factory helper
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def create_trajectory(
    kind: str,
    start: tuple[float, float],
    end: tuple[float, float],
    duration: float = 0.3,
    **kwargs: float,
) -> Trajectory:
    """Create a trajectory by name.

    Args:
        kind: One of ``"slide"``, ``"arc"``, ``"drop"``, ``"flip"``,
            ``"fade"``.
        start: Start position.
        end: End position.
        duration: Total animation time in seconds.
        **kwargs: Extra parameters (e.g. ``arc_height``).

    Returns:
        A :class:`Trajectory` subclass instance.

    Raises:
        ValueError: If *kind* is unknown.
    """
    factories = {
        "slide": SlideTrajectory,
        "arc": ArcTrajectory,
        "drop": DropTrajectory,
        "flip": FlipTrajectory,
        "fade": FadeTrajectory,
    }
    cls = factories.get(kind.lower())
    if cls is None:
        raise ValueError(f"Unknown trajectory kind '{kind}'. Choose from: {', '.join(factories)}")
    return cls(start=start, end=end, duration=duration, **kwargs)  # type: ignore[arg-type]


LinearTrajectory = SlideTrajectory

#engine/core/coordinate_mapper.py
# engine/core/coordinate_mapper.py
"""2D-to-2.5D / Isometric coordinate mapping for board games.

Provides ``GridCoordinateMapper`` — a utility that converts flat board
cell positions ``(col, row)`` into screen-space or isometric 3D
coordinates ``(x, y, z)`` with support for elevation offsets (stacked
pieces, tile thickness).

Usage::

    mapper = GridCoordinateMapper(
        origin=(100, 100),
        cell_size=(64, 64),
        iso_angle=30.0,
    )
    screen_pos = mapper.cell_to_screen(col=3, row=2)
    iso_pos    = mapper.cell_to_iso(col=3, row=2, elevation=1)
    cell       = mapper.screen_to_cell(px=310, py=250)
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math
from dataclasses import dataclass, field


@dataclass
class GridCoordinateMapper:
    """Maps between grid cells and screen / isometric coordinates.

    Attributes:
        origin: Top-left screen origin of the board ``(x, y)``.
        cell_size: Width and height of a single cell ``(w, h)``.
        iso_angle: Isometric tilt angle in degrees (0 = flat top-down).
        elevation_height: Pixel height per elevation unit (for stacking).
        board_rows: Number of rows (needed for inverse mapping).
        board_columns: Number of columns.
    """

    origin: tuple[float, float] = (0.0, 0.0)
    cell_size: tuple[float, float] = (64.0, 64.0)
    iso_angle: float = 0.0
    elevation_height: float = 16.0
    board_rows: int = 8
    board_columns: int = 8

    # Pre-computed trig values (refreshed on angle change)
    _cos_a: float = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _sin_a: float = field(init=False, repr=False)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        self._update_trig()

    def set_iso_angle(self, angle: float) -> None:
        """Change the isometric angle and recompute trig values.

        Args:
            angle: New angle in degrees.
        """
        self.iso_angle = angle
        self._update_trig()

    def _update_trig(self) -> None:
        rad = math.radians(self.iso_angle)
        self._cos_a = math.cos(rad)
        self._sin_a = math.sin(rad)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Cell → Screen (flat, non-isometric)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def cell_to_screen(
        self,
        col: int,
        row: int,
        *,
        center: bool = True,
    ) -> tuple[float, float]:
        """Convert a grid cell to a flat screen position.

        Args:
            col: Column index (0-based, left-to-right).
            row: Row index (0-based, top-to-bottom).
            center: If ``True``, return the centre of the cell;
                otherwise return the top-left corner.

        Returns:
            ``(screen_x, screen_y)``
        """
        cw, ch = self.cell_size
        ox, oy = self.origin
        x = ox + col * cw
        y = oy + row * ch
        if center:
            x += cw * 0.5
            y += ch * 0.5
        return (x, y)

    def screen_to_cell(
        self,
        px: float,
        py: float,
    ) -> tuple[int, int] | None:
        """Convert a screen position to a grid cell.

        Args:
            px: Screen x coordinate.
            py: Screen y coordinate.

        Returns:
            ``(col, row)`` if inside the board, else ``None``.
        """
        cw, ch = self.cell_size
        ox, oy = self.origin
        col = int((px - ox) / cw) if cw > 0 else 0
        row = int((py - oy) / ch) if ch > 0 else 0
        if 0 <= col < self.board_columns and 0 <= row < self.board_rows:
            return (col, row)
        return None

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Cell → Isometric 3D
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def cell_to_iso(
        self,
        col: int,
        row: int,
        elevation: float = 0.0,
        *,
        center: bool = True,
    ) -> tuple[float, float, float]:
        """Convert a grid cell to isometric screen coordinates.

        The isometric projection tilts the Y-axis by ``iso_angle``
        and lifts the point by ``elevation * elevation_height``.

        Args:
            col: Column index.
            row: Row index.
            elevation: Logical elevation units (0 = board surface,
                1 = one piece height above, etc.).
            center: If ``True``, return the centre of the cell.

        Returns:
            ``(iso_x, iso_y, iso_z)`` where ``iso_z`` is the
            visual elevation offset in pixels.
        """
        sx, sy = self.cell_to_screen(col, row, center=center)

        # Apply isometric skew
        iso_x = (sx - sy) * self._cos_a
        iso_y = (sx + sy) * self._sin_a
        iso_z = elevation * self.elevation_height

        # Shift y upward by elevation
        iso_y -= iso_z

        return (iso_x, iso_y, iso_z)

    def board_to_iso(
        self,
        col: int,
        row: int,
        elevation: float = 0.0,
        *,
        center: bool = True,
    ) -> tuple[float, float, float]:
        """Alias for ``cell_to_iso``."""
        return self.cell_to_iso(col, row, elevation, center=center)

    def iso_to_screen(
        self,
        iso_x: float,
        iso_y: float,
        iso_z: float = 0.0,
    ) -> tuple[float, float]:
        """Project isometric coordinates back to flat screen space.

        Args:
            iso_x: Isometric x.
            iso_y: Isometric y.
            iso_z: Isometric z (elevation offset).

        Returns:
            ``(screen_x, screen_y)``
        """
        if abs(self._cos_a) < 1e-10 or abs(self._sin_a) < 1e-10:
            return (iso_x, iso_y + iso_z)

        sx = (iso_x / self._cos_a + (iso_y + iso_z) / self._sin_a) * 0.5
        sy = ((iso_y + iso_z) / self._sin_a - iso_x / self._cos_a) * 0.5
        return (sx, sy)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Board geometry helpers
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def get_board_rect(self) -> tuple[float, float, float, float]:
        """Return the bounding rectangle of the entire board.

        Returns:
            ``(x, y, width, height)`` in screen coordinates.
        """
        ox, oy = self.origin
        cw, ch = self.cell_size
        return (ox, oy, cw * self.board_columns, ch * self.board_rows)

    def get_cell_rect(
        self,
        col: int,
        row: int,
    ) -> tuple[float, float, float, float]:
        """Return the bounding rectangle of a single cell.

        Args:
            col: Column index.
            row: Row index.

        Returns:
            ``(x, y, width, height)``
        """
        cw, ch = self.cell_size
        ox, oy = self.origin
        return (ox + col * cw, oy + row * ch, cw, ch)

    def get_intersection_point(
        self,
        col: int,
        row: int,
    ) -> tuple[float, float]:
        """Return the intersection point for games played on intersections.

        For Go / Gomoku / Pente the pieces sit on the grid line
        intersections rather than inside cells.

        Args:
            col: Column index.
            row: Row index.

        Returns:
            ``(screen_x, screen_y)``
        """
        cw, ch = self.cell_size
        ox, oy = self.origin
        return (ox + col * cw, oy + row * ch)


CoordinateMapper = GridCoordinateMapper

#engine/core/event_manager.py
# engine/core/event_manager.py
"""Lightweight observer / signal system for game events.

Allows any part of the engine or UI to subscribe to named events and
receive notifications with arbitrary payloads.  Designed to decouple
game logic from rendering, sound, and analytics.

Usage::

    em = EventManager()
    em.subscribe(EventType.ON_MOVE_MADE, my_callback)
    em.emit(EventType.ON_MOVE_MADE, move=(0, 0), player=1)

    # Later…
    em.unsubscribe(EventType.ON_MOVE_MADE, my_callback)
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
from collections import defaultdict
from collections.abc import Callable
from typing import Any

from engine.utils.constants import EventType

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# Type alias for listener callbacks
Listener = Callable[..., None]


class EventManager:
    """Central event bus for the MindWar Arena engine.

    Attributes:
        _listeners: Mapping from event type to an ordered list of
            subscriber callbacks.
        _muted: Set of event types that are temporarily suppressed.
        _paused: If ``True`` **all** events are suppressed.
    """

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._listeners: dict[EventType, list[Listener]] = defaultdict(list)
        self._muted: set[EventType] = set()
        self._paused: bool = False

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Subscription
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def subscribe(
        self,
        event_type: EventType,
        callback: Listener,
    ) -> None:
        """Register *callback* to be called when *event_type* fires.

        Args:
            event_type: The event to listen for.
            callback: A callable ``f(**kwargs)`` that will receive the
                event payload as keyword arguments.
        """
        if callback not in self._listeners[event_type]:
            self._listeners[event_type].append(callback)

    def unsubscribe(
        self,
        event_type: EventType,
        callback: Listener,
    ) -> None:
        """Remove *callback* from the listener list of *event_type*.

        Args:
            event_type: The event type.
            callback: The previously registered callback.
        """
        try:
            self._listeners[event_type].remove(callback)
        except ValueError:
            pass

    def unsubscribe_all(
        self,
        event_type: EventType | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        """Remove all listeners for *event_type*, or **all** if ``None``.

        Args:
            event_type: Specific event to clear, or ``None`` for all.
        """
        if event_type is None:
            self._listeners.clear()
        else:
            self._listeners[event_type].clear()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Emission
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def emit(self, event_type: EventType, **kwargs: Any) -> None:
        """Fire an event, notifying all registered listeners.

        Keyword arguments are forwarded to each listener callback as-is.

        Args:
            event_type: The event to emit.
            **kwargs: Arbitrary payload (e.g. ``move``, ``player``).
        """
        if self._paused:
            return
        if event_type in self._muted:
            return

        for callback in list(self._listeners.get(event_type, [])):
            try:
                callback(**kwargs)
            except Exception:
                logger.exception(
                    "Error in event listener %s for %s",
                    callback,
                    event_type,
                )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Mute / Pause
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def mute(self, event_type: EventType) -> None:
        """Temporarily suppress *event_type*.

        Args:
            event_type: The event type to mute.
        """
        self._muted.add(event_type)

    def unmute(self, event_type: EventType) -> None:
        """Resume delivery of *event_type*.

        Args:
            event_type: The event type to unmute.
        """
        self._muted.discard(event_type)

    def pause(self) -> None:
        """Suppress **all** events globally."""
        self._paused = True

    def resume(self) -> None:
        """Resume global event delivery."""
        self._paused = False

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Queries
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def has_listeners(self, event_type: EventType) -> bool:
        """Return ``True`` if *event_type* has at least one subscriber.

        Args:
            event_type: The event type to check.

        Returns:
            ``True`` if listeners exist.
        """
        return bool(self._listeners.get(event_type))

    def listener_count(self, event_type: EventType) -> int:
        """Return the number of listeners for *event_type*.

        Args:
            event_type: The event type to check.

        Returns:
            Non-negative integer.
        """
        return len(self._listeners.get(event_type, []))


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Module-level singleton (optional convenience)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_global_event_manager: EventManager | None = None


def get_event_manager() -> EventManager:
    """Return the global engine-wide :class:`EventManager` singleton.

    Returns:
        The shared event manager instance (created on first call).
    """
    global _global_event_manager
    if _global_event_manager is None:
        _global_event_manager = EventManager()
    return _global_event_manager


def reset_event_manager(replacement: EventManager | None = None) -> None:
    """Clear and optionally replace the global :class:`EventManager`.

    Useful for testing or when reinitialising the engine.

    Args:
        replacement: If provided, use this as the new global instance.
            Otherwise a fresh ``EventManager`` is created on next access.
    """
    global _global_event_manager
    if _global_event_manager is not None:
        _global_event_manager.unsubscribe_all()
    _global_event_manager = replacement

#engine/core/state_manager.py
# engine/core/state_manager.py
"""Undo / Redo state management via an in-place move stack.

Instead of deep-copying the entire board for each move (which is slow
and memory-heavy), the StateManager stores lightweight *MoveRecord*
snapshots that can be replayed forward or backward.

Usage::

    sm = StateManager()
    sm.push(MoveRecord(
        move=(6, 4, 4, 4),
        captured_pieces=[(4, 4, EMPTY)],
        previous_state={"en_passant": None, "castling": {...}},
    ))
    sm.undo()  # pops the last record
    sm.redo()  # replays it
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any


@dataclass
class MoveRecord:
    """A lightweight record of a single move for undo/redo support.

    Stores just enough information to reverse and replay the move
    without needing to deep-copy the full board.

    Attributes:
        move: The move representation (game-specific).
        player: The player who made the move.
        captured_pieces: List of ``(row, col, previous_value)`` tuples
            for any cells that were overwritten (captures, clears).
        previous_state: Snapshot of any game-level state that changed
            (castling rights, en-passant target, ko position, etc.).
        promoted_from: Original piece value before promotion (if any).
        promoted_to: New piece value after promotion (if any).
        extra: Arbitrary game-specific metadata.
    """

    move: Any = None
    player: int = 0
    captured_pieces: list[tuple[int, ...]] = field(default_factory=list)
    previous_state: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
    before_state: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
    after_state: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
    promoted_from: int | None = None
    promoted_to: int | None = None
    extra: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
    metadata: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)


class StateManager:
    """Manages a stack of :class:`MoveRecord` objects for undo/redo.

    Attributes:
        _history: List of applied move records (undo stack).
        _redo_stack: List of undone move records (redo stack).
        _max_history: Maximum number of records to keep.
    """

    def __init__(self, max_history: int = 10_000) -> None:
        self._history: list[MoveRecord] = []
        self._redo_stack: list[MoveRecord] = []
        self._max_history: int = max_history

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Push / Pop
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def push(self, record: MoveRecord) -> None:
        """Push a new move record onto the history stack.

        Clears the redo stack (a new move invalidates the redo timeline).

        Args:
            record: The move record to store.
        """
        self._history.append(record)
        self._redo_stack.clear()

        # Evict oldest entries if over capacity
        if len(self._history) > self._max_history:
            excess = len(self._history) - self._max_history
            del self._history[:excess]

    def record_move(self, record: MoveRecord) -> None:
        """Alias for push."""
        self.push(record)

    def undo(self) -> MoveRecord | None:
        """Pop the most recent move record and push it onto the redo stack.

        Returns:
            The undone :class:`MoveRecord`, or ``None`` if nothing to undo.
        """
        if not self._history:
            return None
        record = self._history.pop()
        self._redo_stack.append(record)
        return record

    def redo(self) -> MoveRecord | None:
        """Re-apply the most recently undone move.

        Returns:
            The redone :class:`MoveRecord`, or ``None`` if nothing to redo.
        """
        if not self._redo_stack:
            return None
        record = self._redo_stack.pop()
        self._history.append(record)
        return record

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Queries
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def can_undo(self) -> bool:
        """Return ``True`` if the history stack is non-empty."""
        return bool(self._history)

    def can_redo(self) -> bool:
        """Return ``True`` if the redo stack is non-empty."""
        return bool(self._redo_stack)

    def peek(self) -> MoveRecord | None:
        """Return the most recent move record without removing it.

        Returns:
            The latest record, or ``None``.
        """
        return self._history[-1] if self._history else None

    def peek_redo(self) -> MoveRecord | None:
        """Return the next redo record without removing it.

        Returns:
            The next redo record, or ``None``.
        """
        return self._redo_stack[-1] if self._redo_stack else None

    @property
    def move_count(self) -> int:
        """Number of moves currently in the history stack."""
        return len(self._history)

    @property
    def redo_count(self) -> int:
        """Number of moves on the redo stack."""
        return len(self._redo_stack)

    def get_history(self) -> list[MoveRecord]:
        """Return a shallow copy of the history stack.

        Returns:
            List of :class:`MoveRecord` objects (oldest first).
        """
        return list(self._history)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Lifecycle
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def clear(self) -> None:
        """Clear both history and redo stacks."""
        self._history.clear()
        self._redo_stack.clear()

    def reset(self) -> None:
        """Alias for :meth:`clear`."""
        self.clear()

#engine/interfaces/ai_interface.py
# engine/interfaces/ai_interface.py
"""Abstract base interface for all AI implementations.

Defines the contract that every game-specific AI must fulfil, including
explicit difficulty tiers and asynchronous move computation.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
from typing import Any

from engine.utils.constants import Difficulty

# Alias for backwards compatibility / readability
AIDifficulty = Difficulty


class AIInterface(ABC):
    """Abstract AI player contract.

    Concrete subclasses live in each game module's ``ai.py`` and
    optionally leverage shared search engines (Minimax, MCTS) from
    ``engine.ai``.
    """

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Lifecycle
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    @abstractmethod
    def initialize(self) -> None:
        """Perform any one-time setup (load tables, warm caches)."""

    @abstractmethod
    def reset(self) -> None:
        """Reset transient state between games."""

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Core
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    @abstractmethod
    def select_action(self, game_state: dict[str, Any]) -> Any | None:
        """Choose a move given the current game state dictionary.

        Args:
            game_state: The dict produced by ``GameInterface.get_state()``.

        Returns:
            A move representation, or ``None`` if no move is available.
        """

    def get_difficulty(self) -> Difficulty:
        """Return the current difficulty level.

        Returns:
            A :class:`Difficulty` enum member.
        """
        return getattr(self, "_difficulty", Difficulty.MEDIUM)

    def set_difficulty(self, difficulty: Difficulty) -> None:
        """Change the AI difficulty level.

        Args:
            difficulty: New difficulty tier.
        """
        self._difficulty = difficulty

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Optional Hooks (override when needed)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def learn(  # noqa: B027
        self,
        state: Any,
        action: Any,
        reward: float,
        next_state: Any,
    ) -> None:
        """Update internal model after observing a transition.

        This is a no-op by default.  Override in reinforcement-learning
        based agents.

        Args:
            state: The state before the action.
            action: The action taken.
            reward: The reward received.
            next_state: The resulting state.
        """

    def evaluate_position(self, game_state: dict[str, Any]) -> float:
        """Return a heuristic evaluation of the current position.

        Higher is better for the maximising player.  The default
        implementation returns ``0.0``.

        Args:
            game_state: The dict produced by ``GameInterface.get_state()``.

        Returns:
            Numeric evaluation score.
        """
        return 0.0

#engine/interfaces/board_interface.py
# engine/interfaces/board_interface.py
"""Abstract base interface for all board representations.

Every game board in MindWar Arena (grid, intersection, or graph) must
implement this interface so the engine, AI, and UI layers can interact
with it in a uniform way.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
from typing import Any


class BoardInterface(ABC):
    """Abstract base class for all board types.

    Subclasses:
        - ``GridBoard``          — row×column cell-based boards
        - ``IntersectionBoard``  — intersection-based boards (Go, Pente)
        - ``GraphBoard``         — node/edge graph boards (Nine Men's Morris)
    """

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Lifecycle
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    @abstractmethod
    def reset(self) -> None:
        """Reset the board to its initial state."""

    @abstractmethod
    def copy(self) -> BoardInterface:
        """Return an independent deep copy of this board.

        Returns:
            A new board instance with identical state.
        """

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Cell / Position Access
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    @abstractmethod
    def get_cell(self, *position: int) -> int:
        """Return the value at the given position.

        Args:
            *position: Position identifier(s) — ``(row, col)`` for grids,
                a single node id for graphs.

        Returns:
            Integer cell value (``EMPTY``, player id, piece id, etc.).
        """

    @abstractmethod
    def set_cell(self, *args: int) -> bool:
        """Set the value at the given position.

        Args:
            *args: Position identifier(s) followed by the value.

        Returns:
            ``True`` if the operation succeeded.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    def is_valid_position(self, *position: int) -> bool:
        """Check whether a position is within bounds.

        Args:
            *position: Position identifier(s).

        Returns:
            ``True`` if valid.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    def is_cell_empty(self, *position: int) -> bool:
        """Check whether the cell at the given position is empty.

        Args:
            *position: Position identifier(s).

        Returns:
            ``True`` if the cell is empty.
        """

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Board Queries
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    @abstractmethod
    def get_board_state(self) -> Any:
        """Return a serialisable snapshot of the current board state.

        Returns:
            A deep-copied representation suitable for hashing or storage.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    def get_board_hash(self) -> int:
        """Return a hash that uniquely identifies the current board state.

        This is used by the transposition table and Superko detection.

        Returns:
            Integer hash value.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    def is_board_full(self) -> bool:
        """Return ``True`` if no empty positions remain."""

    @abstractmethod
    def get_dimensions(self) -> tuple[int, ...]:
        """Return the dimensions of the board.

        Returns:
            Tuple of sizes — e.g. ``(rows, columns)`` or ``(num_nodes,)``.
        """

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Make / Undo (in-place move stack)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    @abstractmethod
    def make_move(self, *args: Any) -> bool:
        """Apply a move in-place and push it onto the undo stack.

        Args:
            *args: Move data — format depends on the game.

        Returns:
            ``True`` if the move was applied successfully.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    def undo_move(self) -> bool:
        """Undo the last move from the internal stack.

        Returns:
            ``True`` if a move was undone.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    def can_undo(self) -> bool:
        """Return ``True`` if there is at least one move to undo."""

    @abstractmethod
    def can_redo(self) -> bool:
        """Return ``True`` if there is at least one move to redo."""

    @abstractmethod
    def redo_move(self) -> bool:
        """Redo the most recently undone move.

        Returns:
            ``True`` if a move was redone.
        """

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Utility
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    @abstractmethod
    def __str__(self) -> str:
        """Human-readable representation of the board."""

    @abstractmethod
    def __eq__(self, other: object) -> bool:
        """Value equality — two boards are equal if their states match."""

    @abstractmethod
    def __hash__(self) -> int:
        """Hash based on the board state."""

#engine/interfaces/game_interface.py
# engine/interfaces/game_interface.py
"""Abstract base interface for all game logic modules.

Every game in MindWar Arena (Tic-Tac-Toe through Go) must implement this
interface.  The expanded contract adds undo/redo, hints, cell-level
querying, and serialisation on top of the original minimal surface.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
from typing import Any


class GameInterface(ABC):
    """Unified contract that each game module must fulfil."""

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Lifecycle
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    @abstractmethod
    def initialize(self) -> None:
        """One-time setup: load resources, create controllers."""

    @abstractmethod
    def reset(self) -> None:
        """Reset to a fresh starting position."""

    @abstractmethod
    def shutdown(self) -> None:
        """Release resources on exit."""

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Frame Loop
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    @abstractmethod
    def update(self) -> None:
        """Called every frame — process player/AI input, advance state."""

    @abstractmethod
    def render(self) -> None:
        """Called every frame — draw the current state to screen."""

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # State Queries
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    @abstractmethod
    def get_state(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """Return a serialisable dictionary of the full game state.

        Returns:
            State dict consumed by AI and serialisation modules.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    def get_board(self) -> Any:
        """Return the board object."""

    @abstractmethod
    def get_current_player(self) -> int:
        """Return the identifier of the player whose turn it is.

        Returns:
            Player id integer.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    def get_winner(self) -> int | None:
        """Return the winning player, or ``None`` if no winner yet.

        Returns:
            Player id or ``None``.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    def is_game_over(self) -> bool:
        """Return ``True`` if the game has ended (win or draw)."""

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Move Execution
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    @abstractmethod
    def make_move(self, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> bool:
        """Validate and apply a move.

        Args:
            *args: Game-specific move representation.
            **kwargs: Optional flags (e.g. ``promotion``).

        Returns:
            ``True`` if the move was successfully applied.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    def get_legal_moves(self) -> list[Any]:
        """Return a list of all legal moves for the current player.

        Returns:
            List of move representations.
        """

    def get_valid_moves(self) -> list[Any]:
        """Alias for get_legal_moves for compatibility."""
        return self.get_legal_moves()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Undo / Redo (optional default implementations)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def can_undo(self) -> bool:
        """Return ``True`` if at least one move can be undone."""
        return False

    def undo(self) -> bool:
        """Undo the last move.

        Returns:
            ``True`` if a move was undone.
        """
        return False

    def can_redo(self) -> bool:
        """Return ``True`` if at least one undone move can be replayed."""
        return False

    def redo(self) -> bool:
        """Redo the most recently undone move.

        Returns:
            ``True`` if a move was redone.
        """
        return False

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Hints & Cell Queries (optional default implementations)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def get_hint(self) -> Any | None:
        """Ask the AI for the best move in the current position.

        Returns:
            A move representation, or ``None`` if unavailable.
        """
        return None

    def get_valid_moves_for_cell(
        self,
        *position: int,
    ) -> list[Any]:
        """Return legal moves originating from a specific cell/position.

        Args:
            *position: Cell identifier(s).

        Returns:
            List of valid move representations.
        """
        all_moves = self.get_legal_moves()
        if not position:
            return all_moves
        result: list[Any] = []
        for move in all_moves:
            if isinstance(move, tuple) and len(move) >= len(position):
                if move[: len(position)] == position:
                    result.append(move)
        return result

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Serialisation (optional default implementations)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def serialize(self) -> str:
        """Serialise the full game state to a portable string.

        The format is game-dependent (FEN for Chess, SGF for Go, etc.).

        Returns:
            String representation of the game state.
        """
        return ""

    def deserialize(self, data: str) -> bool:
        """Restore the game state from a serialised string.

        Args:
            data: Previously serialised state.

        Returns:
            ``True`` if the state was restored successfully.
        """
        return False

#engine/interfaces/game_result.py
# engine/interfaces/game_result.py
"""Rich game result container.

Stores outcome information including winner, draw state, scores,
winning cells/positions, termination reason, and move metadata.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from enum import Enum, auto
from typing import Any


class TerminationReason(Enum):
    """Why the game ended."""

    NONE = auto()
    CHECKMATE = auto()
    STALEMATE = auto()
    RESIGNATION = auto()
    TIMEOUT = auto()
    DRAW_BY_AGREEMENT = auto()
    DRAW_BY_REPETITION = auto()
    DRAW_BY_50_MOVE = auto()
    DRAW_BY_INSUFFICIENT_MATERIAL = auto()
    NO_LEGAL_MOVES = auto()
    ALL_PIECES_CAPTURED = auto()
    CONSECUTIVE_PASSES = auto()  # Go: both players pass
    WIN_BY_CAPTURES = auto()  # Pente: 10 captures
    FIVE_IN_A_ROW = auto()  # Gomoku / Pente
    TERRITORY_SCORING = auto()  # Go: scoring at end


class GameResult:
    """Mutable container for game outcome information.

    Attributes:
        winner: Player id of the winner, or ``None``.
        game_over: ``True`` if the game has ended.
        draw: ``True`` if the game ended in a draw.
        winning_cells: List of board positions forming the winning line/group.
        termination: Reason the game ended.
        scores: Per-player score dictionary (e.g. Go territory).
        last_move: The final move that triggered the result.
        move_count: Total number of half-moves played.
    """

    __slots__ = (
        "winner",
        "game_over",
        "draw",
        "winning_cells",
        "termination",
        "scores",
        "last_move",
        "move_count",
    )

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.winner: int | None = None
        self.game_over: bool = False
        self.draw: bool = False
        self.winning_cells: list[tuple[int, ...]] = []
        self.termination: TerminationReason = TerminationReason.NONE
        self.scores: dict[int, float] = {}
        self.last_move: Any | None = None
        self.move_count: int = 0

    def reset(self) -> None:
        """Clear the result back to a 'game in progress' state."""
        self.winner = None
        self.game_over = False
        self.draw = False
        self.winning_cells.clear()
        self.termination = TerminationReason.NONE
        self.scores.clear()
        self.last_move = None
        self.move_count = 0

    def has_winner(self) -> bool:
        """Return ``True`` if there is a winner."""
        return self.winner is not None

    def is_draw(self) -> bool:
        """Return ``True`` if the game ended in a draw."""
        return self.draw

    def is_game_over(self) -> bool:
        """Return ``True`` if the game has ended."""
        return self.game_over

    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """Serialise the result to a plain dictionary.

        Returns:
            JSON-safe dictionary.
        """
        return {
            "winner": self.winner,
            "game_over": self.game_over,
            "draw": self.draw,
            "winning_cells": list(self.winning_cells),
            "termination": self.termination.name,
            "scores": dict(self.scores),
            "last_move": self.last_move,
            "move_count": self.move_count,
        }

    def __repr__(self) -> str:
        parts = [f"GameResult(over={self.game_over}"]
        if self.winner is not None:
            parts.append(f"winner={self.winner}")
        if self.draw:
            parts.append("draw=True")
        if self.termination != TerminationReason.NONE:
            parts.append(f"reason={self.termination.name}")
        return ", ".join(parts) + ")"

#engine/interfaces/scene_interface.py
# engine/interfaces/scene_interface.py
"""Abstract base interface for all game scenes.

Scenes are the fundamental unit of game flow — each screen the player sees
(boot, menu, gameplay, settings, etc.) is implemented as a Scene.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from engine.game_manager import GameManager


class SceneInterface(ABC):
    """Abstract base class that all scenes must implement.

    A scene has a well-defined lifecycle:
        1. ``enter()``  — called once when the scene becomes active.
        2. ``update()`` — called every frame while active.
        3. ``render()`` — called every frame after update.
        4. ``exit()``   — called once before switching to the next scene.
    """

    @abstractmethod
    def enter(self) -> None:
        """Called once when this scene is activated.

        Use for resource loading, state initialization, etc.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    def exit(self) -> None:
        """Called once when this scene is about to be replaced.

        Use for cleanup, saving state, releasing resources.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    def update(self) -> None:
        """Called once per frame to update logic."""

    @abstractmethod
    def render(self) -> None:
        """Called once per frame to draw visuals."""

    @abstractmethod
    def handle_event(self, event: object) -> None:
        """Handle a raw input or engine event.

        Args:
            event: An engine or pygame event to process.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    def set_game_manager(self, game_manager: GameManager) -> None:
        """Inject the game manager reference.

        Args:
            game_manager: The top-level game manager instance.
        """

#engine/board/grid_board.py
# engine/board/grid_board.py
"""Reusable grid-based board for row×column cell games.

Used by: Tic-Tac-Toe (3×3), Connect Four (6×7), Gomoku (15×15),
Chess (8×8), Checkers (8×8), Othello (8×8).

Features:
    - In-place make/undo move stack (no deep-copy).
    - Zobrist hashing for transposition tables / repetition detection.
    - Frozen (hashable) board snapshots.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import random
from typing import Any

from engine.interfaces.board_interface import BoardInterface


class GridBoard(BoardInterface):
    """A row×column grid board with integer cell values.

    Attributes:
        rows: Number of rows.
        columns: Number of columns.
        empty_value: Value representing an empty cell (default ``0``).
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        rows: int = 8,
        columns: int = 8,
        empty_value: int = 0,
        *,
        num_piece_types: int = 16,
    ) -> None:
        self.rows: int = rows
        self.columns: int = columns
        self.empty_value: int = empty_value

        self._board: list[list[int]] = []
        self._move_stack: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
        self._redo_stack: list[dict[str, Any]] = []

        # Zobrist hashing
        self._zobrist_table: list[list[list[int]]] = []
        self._zobrist_hash: int = 0
        self._num_piece_types: int = num_piece_types
        self._init_zobrist()

        self.reset()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Zobrist Hashing
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _init_zobrist(self) -> None:
        """Initialise the Zobrist random number table."""
        rng = random.Random(42)  # Fixed seed for reproducibility
        self._zobrist_table = [
            [
                [rng.getrandbits(64) for _ in range(self._num_piece_types)]
                for _ in range(self.columns)
            ]
            for _ in range(self.rows)
        ]

    def _compute_zobrist(self) -> int:
        """Recompute the full Zobrist hash from scratch."""
        h = 0
        for r in range(self.rows):
            for c in range(self.columns):
                piece = self._board[r][c]
                if piece != self.empty_value and piece < self._num_piece_types:
                    h ^= self._zobrist_table[r][c][piece]
        return h

    def _update_zobrist(self, row: int, col: int, old_val: int, new_val: int) -> None:
        """Incrementally update the Zobrist hash."""
        if old_val != self.empty_value and old_val < self._num_piece_types:
            self._zobrist_hash ^= self._zobrist_table[row][col][old_val]
        if new_val != self.empty_value and new_val < self._num_piece_types:
            self._zobrist_hash ^= self._zobrist_table[row][col][new_val]

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Lifecycle
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def reset(self) -> None:
        self._board = [[self.empty_value for _ in range(self.columns)] for _ in range(self.rows)]
        self._move_stack.clear()
        self._redo_stack.clear()
        self._zobrist_hash = 0

    def copy(self) -> GridBoard:
        """Return an independent copy (for AI simulation)."""
        new_board = GridBoard(
            self.rows,
            self.columns,
            self.empty_value,
            num_piece_types=self._num_piece_types,
        )
        new_board._board = [row[:] for row in self._board]
        new_board._zobrist_hash = self._zobrist_hash
        new_board._zobrist_table = self._zobrist_table  # Shared (immutable)
        # Don't copy move stacks — copies are for read-only simulation
        return new_board

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Cell Access
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def get_cell(self, row: int, col: int) -> int:
        """Return the value at ``(row, col)``.

        Args:
            row: Row index.
            col: Column index.

        Returns:
            Cell value.
        """
        return self._board[row][col]

    def set_cell(self, row: int, col: int, value: int) -> bool:
        """Set the value at ``(row, col)`` and update the Zobrist hash.

        Args:
            row: Row index.
            col: Column index.
            value: New cell value.

        Returns:
            ``True`` if successful.
        """
        if not self.is_valid_position(row, col):
            return False
        old = self._board[row][col]
        self._board[row][col] = value
        self._update_zobrist(row, col, old, value)
        return True

    def place_piece(self, row: int, col: int, value: int) -> bool:
        """Alias for ``set_cell``."""
        return self.set_cell(row, col, value)

    def is_valid_position(self, row: int, col: int) -> bool:
        return 0 <= row < self.rows and 0 <= col < self.columns

    def is_cell_empty(self, row: int, col: int) -> bool:
        if not self.is_valid_position(row, col):
            return False
        return self._board[row][col] == self.empty_value

    def is_empty(self, row: int, col: int) -> bool:
        """Alias for ``is_cell_empty``."""
        return self.is_cell_empty(row, col)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Board Queries
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def get_board_state(self) -> list[list[int]]:
        """Return a deep copy of the internal 2-D list."""
        return [row[:] for row in self._board]

    def get_board_hash(self) -> int:
        return self._zobrist_hash

    def is_board_full(self) -> bool:
        for row in self._board:
            for cell in row:
                if cell == self.empty_value:
                    return False
        return True

    def get_dimensions(self) -> tuple[int, int]:
        return (self.rows, self.columns)

    def get_available_moves(self) -> list[tuple[int, int]]:
        """Return positions of all empty cells."""
        moves: list[tuple[int, int]] = []
        for r in range(self.rows):
            for c in range(self.columns):
                if self._board[r][c] == self.empty_value:
                    moves.append((r, c))
        return moves

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Make / Undo Move Stack
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def make_move(
        self,
        row: int,
        col: int,
        value: int,
        *,
        extra: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
    ) -> bool:
        """Place *value* at ``(row, col)`` and push onto the undo stack.

        Args:
            row: Row index.
            col: Column index.
            value: Piece / player value.
            extra: Optional extra undo data.

        Returns:
            ``True`` if applied.
        """
        if not self.is_valid_position(row, col):
            return False
        old_value = self._board[row][col]

        record: dict[str, Any] = {
            "row": row,
            "col": col,
            "old_value": old_value,
            "new_value": value,
        }
        if extra is not None:
            record["extra"] = extra

        self.set_cell(row, col, value)
        self._move_stack.append(record)
        self._redo_stack.clear()
        return True

    def undo_move(self) -> bool:
        if not self._move_stack:
            return False
        record = self._move_stack.pop()
        self.set_cell(record["row"], record["col"], record["old_value"])
        self._redo_stack.append(record)
        return True

    def redo_move(self) -> bool:
        if not self._redo_stack:
            return False
        record = self._redo_stack.pop()
        self.set_cell(record["row"], record["col"], record["new_value"])
        self._move_stack.append(record)
        return True

    def can_undo(self) -> bool:
        return bool(self._move_stack)

    def can_redo(self) -> bool:
        return bool(self._redo_stack)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Piece queries
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def count_pieces(self, value: int) -> int:
        """Count cells matching *value*."""
        count = 0
        for row in self._board:
            for cell in row:
                if cell == value:
                    count += 1
        return count

    def find_pieces(self, value: int) -> list[tuple[int, int]]:
        """Return all positions containing *value*."""
        positions: list[tuple[int, int]] = []
        for r in range(self.rows):
            for c in range(self.columns):
                if self._board[r][c] == value:
                    positions.append((r, c))
        return positions

    def find_first(self, value: int) -> tuple[int, int] | None:
        """Return the first position of *value*, or ``None``."""
        for r in range(self.rows):
            for c in range(self.columns):
                if self._board[r][c] == value:
                    return (r, c)
        return None

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Dunder Methods
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def __str__(self) -> str:
        lines: list[str] = []
        for row in self._board:
            lines.append(" ".join(str(c) for c in row))
        return "\n".join(lines)

    def __eq__(self, other: object) -> bool:
        if not isinstance(other, GridBoard):
            return NotImplemented
        return self._board == other._board

    def __hash__(self) -> int:
        return self._zobrist_hash

#engine/board/graph_board.py
# engine/board/graph_board.py
"""Node-and-edge graph board for non-rectangular games.

Used by: Nine Men's Morris (24 nodes connected by edges).

The board is a dictionary mapping node ids to values, plus an
adjacency list defining legal connections.

Features:
    - Arbitrary topology via adjacency lists.
    - Zobrist hashing for transposition tables.
    - In-place make/undo move stack.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import random
from typing import Any

from engine.interfaces.board_interface import BoardInterface


class GraphBoard(BoardInterface):
    """A graph-based board defined by nodes and edges.

    Attributes:
        num_nodes: Total number of positions on the board.
        empty_value: Value for an unoccupied node.
        adjacency: Mapping from node id → list of neighbour node ids.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        num_nodes: int = 24,
        adjacency: dict[int, list[int]] | None = None,
        empty_value: int = 0,
        *,
        num_piece_types: int = 4,
    ) -> None:
        self.num_nodes: int = num_nodes
        self.empty_value: int = empty_value
        self.adjacency: dict[int, list[int]] = adjacency or {}

        self._nodes: dict[int, int] = {}
        self._move_stack: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
        self._redo_stack: list[dict[str, Any]] = []

        # Zobrist
        self._num_piece_types: int = num_piece_types
        self._zobrist_table: dict[int, list[int]] = {}
        self._zobrist_hash: int = 0
        self._init_zobrist()

        self.reset()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Zobrist
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _init_zobrist(self) -> None:
        rng = random.Random(777)
        for node_id in range(self.num_nodes):
            self._zobrist_table[node_id] = [
                rng.getrandbits(64) for _ in range(self._num_piece_types)
            ]

    def _update_zobrist(self, node: int, old_val: int, new_val: int) -> None:
        if old_val != self.empty_value and old_val < self._num_piece_types:
            self._zobrist_hash ^= self._zobrist_table[node][old_val]
        if new_val != self.empty_value and new_val < self._num_piece_types:
            self._zobrist_hash ^= self._zobrist_table[node][new_val]

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Lifecycle
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def reset(self) -> None:
        self._nodes = {i: self.empty_value for i in range(self.num_nodes)}
        self._move_stack.clear()
        self._redo_stack.clear()
        self._zobrist_hash = 0

    def copy(self) -> GraphBoard:
        new_board = GraphBoard(
            self.num_nodes,
            adjacency={k: list(v) for k, v in self.adjacency.items()},
            empty_value=self.empty_value,
            num_piece_types=self._num_piece_types,
        )
        new_board._nodes = dict(self._nodes)
        new_board._zobrist_hash = self._zobrist_hash
        new_board._zobrist_table = self._zobrist_table
        return new_board

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Cell Access  (node-based)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def get_cell(self, node: int, *_: int) -> int:
        """Return the value at the given node.

        Args:
            node: Node id (0-based).

        Returns:
            Node value.
        """
        return self._nodes.get(node, self.empty_value)

    def set_cell(self, node: int, value: int, *_: int) -> bool:
        """Set the value at a node.

        Args:
            node: Node id.
            value: New value.

        Returns:
            ``True`` if valid.
        """
        if not self.is_valid_position(node):
            return False
        old = self._nodes[node]
        self._nodes[node] = value
        self._update_zobrist(node, old, value)
        return True

    def is_valid_position(self, node: int, *_: int) -> bool:
        return 0 <= node < self.num_nodes

    def is_cell_empty(self, node: int, *_: int) -> bool:
        return self._nodes.get(node, self.empty_value) == self.empty_value

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Graph Queries
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def get_neighbors(self, node: int) -> list[int]:
        """Return the list of adjacent node ids.

        Args:
            node: Node id.

        Returns:
            List of neighbour ids.
        """
        return self.adjacency.get(node, [])

    def are_adjacent(self, node_a: int, node_b: int) -> bool:
        """Return ``True`` if *node_a* and *node_b* are connected.

        Args:
            node_a: First node.
            node_b: Second node.

        Returns:
            ``True`` if adjacent.
        """
        return node_b in self.adjacency.get(node_a, [])

    def add_edge(self, node_a: int, node_b: int, bidirectional: bool = True) -> None:
        """Add an edge between two nodes.

        Args:
            node_a: First node id.
            node_b: Second node id.
            bidirectional: If True, also connect node_b -> node_a.
        """
        if node_a not in self.adjacency:
            self.adjacency[node_a] = []
        if node_b not in self.adjacency[node_a]:
            self.adjacency[node_a].append(node_b)

        if bidirectional:
            if node_b not in self.adjacency:
                self.adjacency[node_b] = []
            if node_a not in self.adjacency[node_b]:
                self.adjacency[node_b].append(node_a)

    def get_player_nodes(self, player: int) -> list[int]:
        """Return all nodes occupied by *player*.

        Args:
            player: Player id.

        Returns:
            List of node ids.
        """
        return [n for n, v in self._nodes.items() if v == player]

    def count_pieces(self, player: int) -> int:
        """Count how many nodes are occupied by *player*."""
        return sum(1 for v in self._nodes.values() if v == player)

    def get_empty_nodes(self) -> list[int]:
        """Return all empty nodes."""
        return [n for n, v in self._nodes.items() if v == self.empty_value]

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Board Queries
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def get_board_state(self) -> dict[int, int]:
        return dict(self._nodes)

    def get_board_hash(self) -> int:
        return self._zobrist_hash

    def is_board_full(self) -> bool:
        return all(v != self.empty_value for v in self._nodes.values())

    def get_dimensions(self) -> tuple[int]:
        return (self.num_nodes,)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Make / Undo
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def make_move(
        self,
        node: int,
        value: int,
        *,
        removed_from: int | None = None,
        removed_value: int | None = None,
        extra: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
    ) -> bool:
        """Place or move a piece and record for undo.

        Args:
            node: Target node.
            value: Piece/player value to place.
            removed_from: If a piece was removed from another node
                (e.g. moving a piece), record its old node.
            removed_value: The value that was at ``removed_from``.
            extra: Any additional undo metadata.

        Returns:
            ``True`` if successful.
        """
        if not self.is_valid_position(node):
            return False

        record: dict[str, Any] = {
            "node": node,
            "old_value": self._nodes[node],
            "new_value": value,
        }
        if removed_from is not None:
            record["removed_from"] = removed_from
            record["removed_value"] = (
                removed_value
                if removed_value is not None
                else self._nodes.get(removed_from, self.empty_value)
            )
        if extra:
            record["extra"] = extra

        # Apply removal first
        if removed_from is not None:
            self.set_cell(removed_from, self.empty_value)

        self.set_cell(node, value)
        self._move_stack.append(record)
        self._redo_stack.clear()
        return True

    def undo_move(self) -> bool:
        if not self._move_stack:
            return False
        record = self._move_stack.pop()

        # Restore target node
        self.set_cell(record["node"], record["old_value"])

        # Restore removed piece
        if "removed_from" in record:
            self.set_cell(record["removed_from"], record["removed_value"])

        self._redo_stack.append(record)
        return True

    def redo_move(self) -> bool:
        if not self._redo_stack:
            return False
        record = self._redo_stack.pop()

        if "removed_from" in record:
            self.set_cell(record["removed_from"], self.empty_value)

        self.set_cell(record["node"], record["new_value"])
        self._move_stack.append(record)
        return True

    def can_undo(self) -> bool:
        return bool(self._move_stack)

    def can_redo(self) -> bool:
        return bool(self._redo_stack)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Dunder
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def __str__(self) -> str:
        return " | ".join(f"{n}:{self._nodes[n]}" for n in sorted(self._nodes))

    def __eq__(self, other: object) -> bool:
        if not isinstance(other, GraphBoard):
            return NotImplemented
        return self._nodes == other._nodes

    def __hash__(self) -> int:
        return self._zobrist_hash

#engine/board/intersection_board.py
# engine/board/intersection_board.py
"""Board where pieces are placed on grid line intersections.

Used by: Go (9×9 / 13×13 / 19×19), Gomoku (15×15), Pente (19×19).

In intersection games the ``(row, col)`` coordinate identifies a
line crossing, not the centre of a cell.  The board still stores
values in a 2-D array — the semantic difference is handled by the
coordinate mapper and renderers.

Features:
    - Stone placement / removal.
    - Zobrist hashing (shared base from GridBoard).
    - Position history for Superko detection.
    - In-place make/undo move stack.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import random
from typing import Any

from engine.interfaces.board_interface import BoardInterface


class IntersectionBoard(BoardInterface):
    """A grid-intersection board with Superko-aware position tracking.

    Attributes:
        size: Board size (``size × size``).
        empty_value: Integer representing an empty intersection.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        size: int = 19,
        empty_value: int = 0,
        *,
        num_piece_types: int = 4,
    ) -> None:
        self.size: int = size
        self.rows: int = size
        self.columns: int = size
        self.empty_value: int = empty_value

        self._board: list[list[int]] = []
        self._move_stack: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
        self._redo_stack: list[dict[str, Any]] = []

        # Zobrist hashing
        self._num_piece_types: int = num_piece_types
        self._zobrist_table: list[list[list[int]]] = []
        self._zobrist_hash: int = 0
        self._init_zobrist()

        # Position history for Superko
